The group utilized sophisticated methods, including compromise of software packages and CI/CD pipelines, to spread malware and collect sensitive credentials from targeted systems.

 - TeamPCP has conducted a widespread series of attacks targeting software development processes.
 - The arrests follow a lengthy investigation into the group's operations and their impact on global cybersecurity.

 - The investigation is ongoing to assess the full impact and scope of the breach.

Berlin is experiencing a significant cyber-attack as hackers have compromised city systems, stolen data, and are demanding a ransom of 30 bitcoin. The Rhysida group, believed to operate from Russia and Eastern Europe, claims responsibility for the attack, which has already disrupted city services and potentially exposed personal data. Officials are actively investigating the breach and are refusing to pay the ransom, stating that election infrastructure remains unaffected as elections approach.

 - Hackers have compromised Berlin's city systems and stolen sensitive data.
 - The Rhysida group demands a ransom of 30 bitcoin and threatens to auction the stolen data.

Recent research has revealed that ancient Greek elites crafted jewelry from meteoritic iron over 3,000 years ago, predating widespread iron smelting in the region. A study examining artifacts indicated that meteoritic iron was a prestigious material, specifically used for finger rings associated with high social status. The practice appears to have declined after the 12th century BCE, coinciding with significant societal upheavals during the Late Bronze Age collapse.

 - The study identified 9 ancient rings likely made from meteoritic iron, showcasing its rarity and value in ancient Greek society.
 - The rings were predominantly found in elite tombs, suggesting a connection between meteoritic iron and social power among the Minoan and Mycenaean civilizations.

Source: https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/world/europe/ancient-greek-elites-wore-jewellery-made-from-metal-that-fell-from-space-scientists-identified-meteorite-iron-in-3000-year-old-rings/articleshow/133598951.cms

A near-total lunar eclipse occurred on August 27, 2026, where the moon appeared red-orange due to Earth's shadow covering it. The event was visible from North America, Europe, and Africa, peaking at 12:13 A.M. EDT when over 96 percent of the moon was obscured. The next total lunar eclipse will not take place until December 31, 2028, while the year 2027 will feature two solar eclipses.

 - The lunar eclipse transformed the moon into a red-orange 'blood moon' as over 96 percent was covered by Earth's shadow.
 - The next total lunar eclipse will be on December 31, 2028, and 2027 will see two solar eclipses.

Source: https://www.scientificamerican.com/article/see-last-nights-blood-moon-eclipse-from-around-the-world/

In Ceuta, Spain, local residents have begun protests against the government's handling of a sudden influx of migrants, demanding removals from public areas. The situation escalated as citizens confronted authorities and engaged in actions to dismantle makeshift camps on local beaches, leading to clashes with police who used rubber bullets against protesters. Tensions remain high as residents express feelings of abandonment by the government amidst deteriorating living conditions.

The FBI Director is criticizing a directive from CBC News that instructs journalists to avoid using the term "terrorist attacks" when reporting on the 9/11 events. This policy has received backlash from U.S. officials and some Canadian politicians, who argue that it minimizes the impact of the attacks carried out by al-Qaeda.

 - The FBI Director condemned CBC's directive for not labeling the 9/11 attacks as terrorist acts.
 - Canadian politicians have criticized the CBC for this policy, arguing it disrespects the victims and downplays the significance of the attacks.

Category: Technology
Importance: 6
Wall Street analysts have warned that a public backlash against smart glasses, particularly Meta's Ray-Ban models, could hinder the growth of the AI-wearables industry. Sales of these glasses surged in 2025, but rising privacy concerns and social media backlash have drastically changed public opinion, labeling them as 'pervert glasses.' Advocacy groups are campaigning for changes in regulations to ensure the glasses are identifiable, impacting companies like EssilorLuxottica financially and reputationally.

 - Public backlash against smart glasses is increasing due to privacy concerns.
 - Sales of Meta's Ray-Ban glasses saw a rise, but public opinion has since turned negative.
 - Advocacy groups are pushing for clearer identification rules for smart glasses.

Source: https://www.zerohedge.com/technology/wall-street-warns-pervert-glasses-backlash-threatens-ai-wearables-boom
